Pronunciation respellings: Sephardi

Pack 05b · 1333 rows · best first reviewer: Cantor or rabbi (liturgy and trope) · nothing in this pack has been reviewed by a native speaker or clergy; the app text is a draft.
Write corrections in the correction column (or in the matching CSV). Do not rewrite the Hebrew column. The CSV with the same name is where answers are returned.
How to mark (all reviewers)
  • Read each Hebrew cell aloud before you decide.
  • In the verdict column write OK (you would put your name to it), Fix (and write the right text in the correction column), or Unsure (say why in notes).
  • Leave a row blank if you did not look at it. Blank means "not reviewed" and that is fine.
  • Do not change the id or Hebrew columns. Corrections go in the correction column only.
  • Return the file as CSV (UTF-8), not .xlsx. Keep the first row (the column names).

What to judge: Pronunciation respellings per tradition

Hebrew teacher (main reviewer for this pack)
Judge:
  • Respelling follows the pointing by the rules of the named tradition
  • Stress position
  • Consistent vowel and consonant choices
Do not judge:
  • Which tradition the app should offer
  • The spelling system itself (only whether it conveys the sound)
Cantor or rabbi (liturgy and trope) (main reviewer for this pack)
Judge:
  • Sephardi and Ashkenazi rows: does a congregation of that tradition say it this way (kamatz, tav, cholam, stress)
  • Prayer-word rows especially
Do not judge:
  • Israeli rows
  • Disputes about which Ashkenazi or Sephardi sub-tradition is "right"; say which one you judge by in notes
Israeli native speaker (modern usage) (main reviewer for this pack)
Judge:
  • Israeli rows: matches how the word is said in Israel today
  • Phrasebook rows: natural everyday sound
Do not judge:
  • Ashkenazi and Sephardi rows (skip unless you know the tradition)
